#7318. Growth Copywriter at Winona

Winona is one of the leading telemedicine companies providing HRT for women in menopause. We’ve built all of our technology in house including an EHR, patient care admin system, patient portal, website, marketing technology etc. Winona has two compounding pharmacies and a team of in house physicians providing world-class care via a fully vertically integrated business model.

We are looking for a data-backed, empathetic Growth Copywriter to own the performance and optimization of our end-to-end messaging ecosystem. At Winona, copy isn’t just about sounding nice. It’s a measurable variable in our growth funnel. You will design and execute rapid copy experiments across our paid acquisition, intake flows, and lifecycle retention loops to help women confidently access bioidentical HRT and menopause care.

Full-Funnel Experimentation. Write copy hypotheses, design rapid A/B tests alongside growth and analytics teams, analyze performance data, and turn winning variations into a scalable messaging playbook.

Lifecycle Growth & Retention. Write, test, and optimize automated onboarding, activation, adherence, churn-save, and win-back sequences across email, SMS, and push notifications to drive LTV.

High-Intent Conversion Surfaces. Own the persuasive strategy and microcopy for landing pages, interactive quiz/intake flows, checkouts, and dynamic pricing/offer messaging.

Paid Acquisition at Scale. Generate volume-driven concepts, hooks, and static/video copy variants for Meta, Google, and YouTube, systematically iterating based on creative testing data.

Voice of Customer (VoC) Intelligence. Continuously mine product reviews, customer support tickets, intake data, and exit surveys to isolate deep qualitative insights and high-converting customer language.

AI-Augmented Workflows. Leverage and train Winona’s internal AI copywriting tools to scale production velocity, maintain brand consistency, and build automated variant pipelines without sacrificing quality.

5+ years of dedicated copywriting experience specializing in direct-response, conversion copywriting, or growth marketing.

DTC Telehealth, Wellness, or HealthTech experience is highly preferred. You understand how to write about sensitive health topics with clinical authority and deep empathy.

A portfolio backed by data and results — the exact copy variants you tested, the structural or psychological changes you made, and the quantifiable lift they produced in metrics like CTR, quiz completion, or conversion rates.

Deep lifecycle marketing mastery: significant experience building and optimizing automated email and SMS flows (onboarding, retention, win-backs), rather than just writing one-off promotional campaigns. You know how to nurture a lead over time.

A strong foundation in A/B testing mechanics — comfortable formulating clear copy hypotheses, understanding sample size requirements, and knowing when a test result has achieved true statistical significance.

The balance of empathy and compliance: a proven ability to write about highly sensitive personal health topics (menopause symptoms, hormonal changes, wellness) with profound empathy while strictly adhering to healthcare compliance rules and ad-network medical restrictions.

An experimenter’s ego — you don’t take copy critiques personally. You’re obsessed with letting consumer behavior and performance data crown the winning angle.

As a full-time member of our team, you’ll enjoy:

Paid non-working holidays per country of residence

Medical/ Dental/ Vision insurance (US-W2)

Employee engagement activities and virtual gatherings

#7310. 5 Questions For…CNBC’s Eamon Javers on His Call With President Trump on Jensen Huang’s Nvidia Reinstatement

What should have been a slow news week with the Thanksgiving holiday approaching was turned on its head Monday morning, when news broke that Nvidia had reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO.

This comes after Huang recently stepped down following revelations of multiple extramarital relationships, including with a woman alleged to be a Chinese spy.

CNBC was one of the first networks to break the news at 8:56 a.m. ET, and was also the first to speak with President Donald Trump following the reinstatement.

This is the moment the Jensen Huang news broke on @CNBC: pic.twitter.com/YKrRxLt7Ul

— Brian Stelter (@brianstelter) November 23, 2026

Eamon Javers, CNBC’s senior Washington correspondent, was the one who initially talked to the president, telling TVNewser that Trump called him as he was headed to CNBC’s DC bureau.

In a follow-up to our recent 5 Questions For… series, which covered Huang’s unexpected departure, Javers details how he learned of the reinstatement, his conversation with the president, and the coordination involved with the control room.

How did you hear about the news of Jensen Huang’s return?

I was walking my daughter to her bus stop this morning, and my phone blew up with texts from our bureau. It’s maybe the most important business story of the year, and I was a little frustrated that I was out of position when it broke. So I began texting sources for comment.

TVNewser: How did the call with President Trump occur?

Javers: He called me as I was in my car on the way to our CNBC bureau. I wasn’t expecting it at all. But we have talked on the phone from time to time, and I had tried to reach him earlier in the morning for his comment. So, I pulled over on the side of the road, called my desk, and was able to patch into the control room for a live interview. The whole process took about 4 minutes, and then we went live for nearly an hour. Our producers, control room team, and anchors did an incredible job of rolling with it and reacting very, very quickly so we could make it happen.

TVN: What was it like to talk to the president in real time during such a unique moment?

Javers: It’s always an honor to talk to the president of the United States, and today it was a unique opportunity because there was so much that was unknown about the role he played behind the scenes. I was focused on all of the aspects that were still unknown, and trying to get the president to explain the who, what, when, and why of how this happened.

TVN: How were you coordinating the conversation with the newsroom and control room?

Javers: We didn’t coordinate it or prep in advance at all. It happened too quickly. And your audience will understand the technical aspect of this, which is that on a phoner, you can’t communicate with the control room during the interview. On TV, they can speak to you in your earpiece to communicate how long you have left, if the anchors want to get in a question, and all of the production stuff you need in real time. Here it was just — do it live.

TV: What are the business ramifications of such a re-appointment?

Javers: We’ve really never seen anything this extraordinary in corporate America — at least in my lifetime. I think it’s too early to say how this will play out, and certainly too early to say whether it will be successful or not. One thing is for sure: This story isn’t over yet.

Mark Mwachiro is a TVNewser contributor who has been writing for the blog since March 2022. Based out of New York, Mark has also contributed to ADWEEK.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

This was the second week in a row World News Tonight had over 9 million total viewers

#7309. Senior Forward Deployed Engineer - Toronto Remote at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

ProcessOS is a new kind of Camunda product: business processes described in natural language, generated as executable BPMN workflows, and improved autonomously from production data. We’re hiring the first cohort of Forward Deployed Engineers to bring it into production at enterprise customers. You’ll own the deployment journey from first process file to a working, improving system, and hand off a functioning capability to the customer’s team. The code you write and the patterns you discover feed directly back into how ProcessOS is built. This is a rare opportunity to shape a product, a function, and a field practice from day one.

You’ll work end‑to‑end across Java services and React interfaces, collaborating closely with a talented global team to measure, improve, and scale performance and fault tolerance in real-world distributed systems.

Deploy and prove. Own the journey from first process file to production workflow. At 90 days: at least one workflow running, the improvement loop active, and measurable fitness gains the customer can see. At 12 months: 3–5 customers with viable deployments.

Drive organizational adoption. Work across technical and business stakeholders to build understanding and trust, turning early deployments into lasting customer capability.

Work at depth technically. Configure and run Camunda clusters locally and in cloud environments. Debug integration failures. Build custom agents where platform defaults don’t fit. Write connector logic that gets legacy enterprise systems talking to ProcessOS.

Build the playbook. There is no deployment template yet. Document what good looks like as you discover it, and make the next implementation faster.

Feed the product. Custom agents you write get contributed back. Integration patterns get codified. Production bugs you find, you often fix. Your field work is a direct input to ProcessOS.

Hand off deliberately. Leave customers with a team that can operate independently, is trained on the platform, and acts on improvement results without you.

Enterprise production experience: you’ve shipped something inside a large organization and stayed to see it operate. You know why governance and resilience matter at scale.

Technical depth: You can debug an integration failure, write a custom agent against a legacy API, stand up a Camunda cluster from scratch, and explain generated BPMN to both engineers and business leads.

Stakeholder communication: you work effectively across engineering, architecture, and business leadership and adapt how you communicate without losing precision.

Practical AI experience: you’ve used AI to solve real problems in production – not just experimented. You understand context management, prompt engineering, agent testing, and integration tradeoffs.

Comfort with ambiguity: you make sound judgment calls without a playbook and document what you learn.

Experience in a customer-facing engineering or professional services role.

Familiarity with regulated industries such as financial services, healthcare, or insurance

Production experience with agentic systems or AI-integrated workflows.

Contributions to a shared platform or open source project that others built on.

#7304. Senior Forward Deployed Engineer at Clutch

Clutch builds AI agents that work on behalf of credit unions. Emma, our collections agent, is live at five credit unions and handled 60,000+ member conversations in her first twelve weeks at one of them. Behind her is a growing portfolio: origination, fraud step-up, member (re)engagement and activation agents, all built on the same Agents Platform (TypeScript, NestJS, our own agent SDK, MCP, Temporal, promptfoo).

As a FDE you are one of the two engineers on an agent. You take it from "sold" to "live and performing" at a specific credit union: you sit in the discovery sessions, you build the integrations to their core banking and loan systems, you author and tune the agent's behavior, you run the live tests with their staff, and you stay with it through go-live and the first weeks of production. When something needs a person in the room, you are the one who flies.

This is a senior engineering role, not a solutions or support role. You write production code in our monorepo every week: integrations, modules, and the agents themselves (prompts, tools, evals), together with the other engineers on the pod. The difference from a traditional engineer is that you are on site more often, you make judgment calls in front of the customer, and you own the outcome. What you learn in the field flows straight back into the product: defaults, playbooks, and the platform capabilities the next agent will need.

You build; our AI Operations Managers deliver. The pod (pod lead plus engineers) designs and builds each agent in a high-touch way with the first customers. Once the agent is defined and live, AI Ops coordinates the implementations, configuration and ongoing success at the next credit unions. You hand them something that works and a playbook to run it.

This role requires roughly 20% of travel on average, with one- to two-week stretches around kickoffs and go-lives. Based in the US, able to travel on short notice

You'll join the Agents team, a small, senior group operating as a startup inside Clutch: seven engineers, a dedicated AI product group, and AI sales specialists, working directly with some of the largest credit unions in the country. Every agent is staffed by a pair of engineers, one leaning toward the conversation and LLM side, one toward software and integrations, with a pod lead who owns the product day to day. There is no layer between you and the customer, and no layer between you and the platform team.

The operating style is direct, written, and evidence-driven: BLUF updates, evals over opinions, business outcomes over ticket counts. The team is spread across Brazil, Argentina and the US and works async by default. Being on site is a tool we use at specific moments (kickoff, live testing, go-live), not a permanent posting.

Ship your first production change to a live agent through the team's review process, and be able to explain how a member's call flows through the Agents Platform end to end

Learn one agent deeply: system prompt, tool contracts, orchestration triggers, compliance gates, and the implementation process from kickoff to go-live

Join at least one credit union implementation as the second engineer, including a live testing session with their staff

Map where the current implementation process loses time and where a default or a tool would have removed a round trip

Own the technical side of one credit union implementation end to end: core and LOS integration, agent configuration, testing, go-live, and the first weeks of production

Run the eval and tuning loop for your agent (golden transcripts, rubric judges, regression suite) and be trusted to change its behavior without a second engineer checking your work

Have completed at least two on-site visits and turned what you heard there into product decisions with your pod lead and PM

Contribute at least one reusable capability back to the Agents Platform that the next implementation will not have to rebuild

Have taken one net-new agent from sold to live at its first credit union, with data you can show the partner

Be the engineer credit union staff ask for by name when something in their deployment needs to change

Have written the implementation playbook for your agent well enough that the AI Ops team runs the second and third credit union without you

Be shaping what the next agents look like, because you have seen where the current ones break in the field

7+ years of software engineering in a fast-paced environment, preferably in SaaS and/or Fintech, with senior TypeScript and Node.js backend experience in a large codebase (NestJS, CQRS, schema-validated boundaries, dependency injection). We do not count years of AI experience; the field is too young for that to mean anything. We look at what you have shipped and how you reason about it

Integration and API engineering: secure service-to-service endpoints, third-party APIs, webhooks, and the patience to work with legacy banking systems that were not designed to be integrated

Security and auth judgment: OAuth and machine-to-machine auth, tenant-scoped access, secrets handling, no PII in logs. Our agents touch sensitive member data and operate under consumer-contact regulation

Customer presence: you can sit across from a VP of Collections, hear a request, and say "here is what I recommend and why", including when the answer is no. You are comfortable making calls in the room and owning them afterwards

Code-quality judgment under deadline pressure: you take on debt deliberately, name it, and pay it down, rather than gold-plating or leaving silent debt behind

Genuine appetite for LLM agents: eval discipline for non-deterministic systems, reading a system prompt like code, understanding that some decisions must never reach the model. Curiosity plus shipped work is enough; research depth is not required

Nice to have, explicitly not required: voice and telephony (LiveKit, Twilio), Temporal or event-driven orchestration, Python, core banking or LOS integration experience, credit union or banking domain knowledge

Remote Flexibility: Enjoy the freedom of remote work from anywhere, balancing life and career seamlessly.

Unforgettable Off-Sites: Twice a year, bond with colleagues in exciting destinations, fostering teamwork and fresh ideas.

Paid Time Off: Enjoy flexible PTO days yearly for relaxation and rejuvenation.

Stock Options: Joining us means having a stake in our success, so you'll receive stock options as part of your compensation package.

Home Office Setup: Create your ideal workspace with a dedicated budget for home office essentials.

Work Trip Budget: Grow personally and professionally with a budget for work-related trips and co-working.

Health Coverage: Prioritize your well-being with comprehensive health and dental insurance plans.

Clutch is a revolutionary vertical SaaS company, proudly backed by Andreessen Horowitz (A16z), aimed at revolutionizing the way Credit Unions engage and change the lives of their members. As a champion of financial well-being, we address the urgent need for affordable lending solutions in an era where the average American grapples with over $155,000 in household debt. Unlike traditional financial institutions, Clutch develops software to turn Credit Unions into FinTech lenders and leverage their balance sheets to responsibly lend to over 130M Americans. Our mission extends beyond mere financial transactions; we strive to fundamentally enhance the way credit unions interact with their members. By integrating cutting-edge technologies and user-centric designs, we help credit unions provide seamless digital experiences that are on par with leading tech companies. This approach not only preserves but revitalizes the longstanding tradition of community and member-focused service inherent to credit unions.

Clutch is an equal opportunity employer. All applicants will be considered for employment without attention to race, color, religion, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, national origin, veteran or disability status.

The Staff GTM Systems Engineer at LiveKit is the technical owner and architect of the systems that power our go-to-market engine. You'll decide how our GTM stack is designed, what we build versus buy, and where we invest next — and you'll be hands-on building it: Salesforce architecture, integrations, enrichment pipelines, and the agentic workflows that help Sales and Marketing move faster and smarter.

Salesforce is our system of record for revenue, pipeline, and customer data. HubSpot is our marketing layer for campaigns and lead generation, and what it captures needs to flow cleanly and reliably into Salesforce. Around them sits a growing stack of enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and data platform tools. You'll own the architecture that ties it all together: disciplined change management, observable pipelines, data models built for AI consumption, and agentic workflows that act on GTM signals at scale — designed to serve the business for years, not quarters.

This role sits at the intersection of GTM, Data, Finance, and Engineering. You'll work directly with senior leaders to shape the GTM systems roadmap, turn business strategy into technical plans, and guide decisions that span multiple teams. You'll also help grow the engineers around you, setting the technical standards and practices the team builds on.

You understand the difference between owning a system and gatekeeping one. Sales lives in Salesforce. Marketing lives in HubSpot. Your job is to make sure both are governed well enough that others can work in them effectively, and powerful enough that the GTM team can do things they couldn't do before.

You'll thrive as a Staff GTM Systems Engineer if you:

Are known as the go-to person for tough technical problems, and have good judgment about which ones matter most.

Think in systems and long time horizons, and can make confident architectural decisions with incomplete information.

Can clearly explain complex technical concepts and tradeoffs to engineers and executives alike.

Believe systems should be managed with engineering discipline.

Ship fast and reliably — your work holds up in production.

Are a fast learner, frequently picking up new languages and tools.

Find real satisfaction in mentoring other engineers and helping them grow.

Own the architecture of the GTM systems stack: define how Salesforce, HubSpot, enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and the data platform fit together, and write the technical strategy that guides how it evolves.

#7293. The Analog Advantage: Luxury Retail in the Age of AI at Bucherer

No one needs a mechanical watch, but then again, luxury is not a category built on need. And that is exactly what makes the marketing challenge interesting.

The value comes from everything surrounding the object: scarcity, craftsmanship, identity, memory, access, and the community someone enters by owning it.

On this episode of The Speed of Culture podcast, Carina Ertl, chief marketing officer at Bucherer USA, joins Matt Britton to explore how those forces shape modern luxury marketing.

Their conversation examines why the category often reverses traditional demand economics, and how experience creates loyalty long after the purchase.

The also discuss why passionate curators can outperform creators with massive followings, and why AI may make human taste an even stronger competitive advantage.

Carina Ertl is the Chief Marketing Officer at Bucherer USA, part of the Swiss luxury watch and jewelry retailer whose heritage dates back to 1888. Bucherer operates across physical retail and ecommerce, offering luxury timepieces and jewelry.

Carina’s career spans luxury brand building, digital transformation, entrepreneurship, private equity, and nearly a decade at Swarovski. Her experience across markets and business models has shaped an approach to marketing built around curiosity, cultural fluency, and long-term client relationships

02:52 Luxury Flips the Demand Curve — Carina describes luxury marketing as “art and science,” with perhaps a little more art than science. Unlike many categories trying to create demand, luxury often deals with the opposite problem: too much demand for too little product. That scarcity changes the marketing playbook. The goal, now, is to protect desirability while deepening the meaning surrounding the product.

06:39 Certified Pre-Owned Turns Trust Into Value — The secondary watch market is growing, but Carina draws a sharp distinction between generic resale and certified pre-owned. Authentication matters because counterfeiting creates real uncertainty in the category. Certification gives consumers confidence while allowing retailers to unlock the history and storytelling embedded in older pieces, turning resale into its own desirable luxury experience.

11:53 Between Product and Sale: Where the Real Product Lies — For Bucherer, the customer journey does not move directly from product to transaction. Carina sees the experience in between as part of the product itself: community, belonging, identity, storytelling, trust, and eventually repurchase. That makes luxury a long-term relationship business rather than a sequence of isolated sales.

14:27 Trust Always Beats Follower Count — Carina says some of Bucherer’s strongest creator partnerships come from passionate curators rather than influencers with millions of followers. A smaller voice with deep category knowledge and a highly engaged community can carry more credibility than broad reach alone. Therein lies a big lesson for marketers: authority and authentic passion can matter more than audience size.

17:36 AI Makes Human Taste More Valuable — Carina is enthusiastic about AI as a productivity and process tool, but she sees a limit when automation starts replacing cultural judgment. In luxury, anyone can copy a formula. The differentiators are taste, creativity, execution, and knowing what is happening in culture and subculture. As AI makes generic ideas easier to generate, the distinctly human parts of brand building become more important.

21:44 Curiosity Survives Every Marketing Shift — Carina’s advice to future marketing leaders is built around adaptability. Marketing has changed repeatedly throughout her career, from digital transformation to AI, and she does not believe anyone can graduate knowing everything they will need. The durable advantage is curiosity: seek unfamiliar projects, learn the broader business, and stay willing to change as the function changes around you.

Matt Britton is America’s leading expert on the millennial generation having consulted for over half of the Fortune 500 over the past two decades. He is the Founder and CEO of Suzy and the host of The Speed of Culture Podcast.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

#7284. Mission Over Metrics: Rethinking the Shopper Journey with Liz Roche

Retail media is no longer just about putting products in front of shoppers and measuring the resulting sales.

As the retail landscape becomes increasingly complex, brands need to rethink how they connect with consumers across the entire shopper journey.

The opportunity lies in building connected campaigns that bring together shopper insights, creative strategy, and coordinated experiences across digital, social, and in-store touchpoints.

In this episode of Adspeak by ADWEEK, host Zoë Ruderman sits down with Liz Roche, VP of media, measurement, and in-store solutions at Albertsons Media Collective, to explore how brands and retailers can move beyond traditional retail media strategies to create more meaningful shopper connections.

They discuss why understanding shopper missions and need states matters more than simply showcasing products, and why shopper data should inform creative development from the very beginning of a campaign.

The conversation examines what makes a genuinely connected campaign. Rather than repeating the same message across multiple channels, brands need to understand where shoppers are in their journey and build experiences that reflect their immediate needs.

Liz explains how seemingly ordinary purchase signals can reveal valuable shopper insights, helping marketers understand the motivations behind buying behavior and make smarter decisions about creative, SKU selection, and promotional strategy.

They also explore the importance of moving beyond vanity metrics like return on ad spend. By combining incrementality, category insights, and brand performance, marketers can develop more meaningful measurement frameworks that demonstrate the true impact of retail media.

The discussion also looks at the untapped potential of the physical store as a media canvas, from in-store screens to audio experiences that can extend brand storytelling beyond traditional advertising.

Liz Roche is the VP of Media, Measurement & In-Store Solutions at Albertsons Media Collective. A seasoned executive, she specializes in first-party data strategy, media measurement, monetization, commercialization, and go-to-market strategy.

With leadership experience spanning platforms, agencies, and publishers, Liz drives business transformation through innovation, strategic partnerships, and collaboration. She holds an Executive MBA from the Kellogg School of Management, where she was honored as the commencement speaker in June 2024.

#7254. The RX Assistant Wants to Help Pharmaceutical Brands Use AI to Reach Patients

On November 17, The Rx Assistant, an AI-powered service that helps patients streamline the prescriptions process by finding doctors, verifying insurance coverage, and enrolling patients in savings programs, announced the launch of Builder — a specialized platform for pharmaceutical brands to deliver AI experiences for patients.

The Rx Assistant Builder is a no-code platform that enables pharmaceutical brands to rapidly create, get regulatory approval for, and deploy compliant AI patient and provider support tools across web, voice, SMS, and email.

The aim of Builder was to further simplify the prescription process for both pharmaceutical manufacturers and patients. From a pharmaceutical brand standpoint, Builder helps gather different tools and processes into one place. The flexibility of user experience, such as being able to switch from online to voice chat, allows manufacturers to cater to different needs depending on different patient populations.

“AI, when deployed correctly, can decrease time wasted in the ‘healthcare consideration cycle.’” said Jake Coniglio, co-founder and CEO, The Rx Assistant. “This counter balancing force can simultaneously allow patients and HCPs to to source information faster and go deeper.”

In its early deployments, The Rx Assistant found that its AI assistant outperformed brands’ own websites on a 3 to 1 scale, on tasks such as finding doctors and completing symptom quizzes. Brands were also able to move from contract signing to approval in eight weeks.

“By deploying our platform to both patients and healthcare providers we can remove a lot of friction on both ends of the journey. With that in mind, we want to be where people are already going and help make that experience more meaningful.” said Coniglio.

“Brands spend millions on patient support programs that patients don’t end up using in large part because they are unaware. We can be the glue that links these disparate silos together.”

Product Specialists and the Support team, who handle the front line and escalate deliverability issues to you

Project: build a recurring report showing the trending deliverability ticket reasons so we understand what’s causing how to reduce friction for customers and how to solve it

Own compliance end to end, from new-account approvals and compliance flags to pausing or closing accounts that put our shared reputation at risk.Run Kit's abuse desk. You'll write and share a strategy that covers coverage, response-time targets, escalation paths, and quality checks, then put it in place so routine spam, phishing, and blocklist reports follow clear SOPs while you take the hardest cases yourself. Be the go-to contact for day-to-day mailbox provider issues with Microsoft, Gmail, Yahoo, Comcast, and regional providers, and write an incident runbook for blocklistings and provider throttling. Track the queue with a simple scorecard (volume, first response time, and escalation rate) and share what you learn with the team. Run training sessions for Support and ship your first process or AI improvements, like cleaner ticket categorization or better account data for our AI support agent, so more deliverability tickets get solved without escalation..

First Year: Deliverability support is faster and more consistent for every creator on Kit, including the ones who never talk to a CGM. You're helping shape the internal tooling and alerting our in-house sending needs, and you take part in industry groups like M3AAWG.

#7243. Senior Analytics Engineer at Kit

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. For coaches, YouTubers, authors, podcasters, and other creatives, there isn't a better marketing hub to rely on to grow audiences, automate email marketing, and sell digital products — all within one platform.More importantly, there isn't a team more committed to helping creators earn a living. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

Our Sales team needs a dedicated data partner, and creator commerce (how creators monetize by selling digital products) is becoming a bigger part of how Kit and our creators scale. Kit is growing, so we're looking for a Senior Analytics Engineer to own Kit's sales and commerce data from end to end.

Accurate sales data gives our team a clear picture of the pipeline and the confidence to make informed decisions. That’s what makes this role so important.

Your primary focus will be Sales: the dbt models that turn our CRM data into trusted pipeline, deal, and funnel metrics, and the Omni dashboards our sales leader and Senior Revenue Operations Manager use to run the business. You'll also model creator commerce data, so we understand what creators sell and earn through Kit, and you'll pick up reporting work with our Product team, such as feature usage reporting. On a small data team, we step outside our usual lanes when the need is there, so the mix will shift over time.

You'll build for two kinds of consumers: people and AI. Teammates increasingly answer their own questions by querying our semantic layer through Omni's AI and MCP connections to AI tools. That means your models, metric definitions, and documentation need to be clear and correct enough for an AI agent to use them without a data teammate in the loop.

This is a full-time individual contributor role for someone who likes being close to the business, enjoys turning messy operational data into numbers people trust, and is happy to work on both the models and the dashboards that sit on top of them. When Sales and Product have clear, reliable data, we help more creators build more valuable businesses with Kit.

You'll report to Sam Umachi, Head of Data (Linkedin).

Sales and RevOps are your primary partners, who will rely on the models and reporting you own. You'll also partner with our Product team on reporting for features like Visual Automations and on how creators use Kit.

Within our data team, you'll work alongside a Staff Analytics Engineer who owns org-wide semantic layer governance and modeling standards, a Senior Data Engineer who owns ingestion and pipelines, and a Senior Business Analytics Partner focused on revenue opportunities. This role has no direct reports.

First Week: Work through our onboarding curriculum in Notion and meet the data team, our Senior Revenue Operations Manager, the sales team, and your product partners. Get set up in our core tools: dbt Cloud, GitHub, Amazon Redshift, Omni, HubSpot, Linear, Hightouch and Slack. Set up your AI-assisted development environment and learn the shared AI workflows our data team uses. Open and merge your first small pull request so you learn our review and deployment flow early.

First Month: Map how HubSpot objects (deals, pipelines and stages, companies, contacts, and owners) flow into our dbt models, and document the gaps. Take over day-to-day ownership of the sales models and Omni dashboards that teammates have been covering. Audit current sales reporting and list where definitions differ or numbers don't reconcile. Get familiar with how commerce data (products creators sell and the revenue they earn through Kit) lands in the warehouse today. Agree with RevOps and Sales on the most important questions they need answered. Review teammates' pull requests and contribute to our shared SQL, dbt, and documentation standards. Share a written reflection with the team on what you've learned and where you'd focus first.

First Six Months: Ship and maintain well-tested, documented dbt models for core sales metrics, such as pipeline, deal velocity, stage conversion, win rates, and the sales-led funnel. Expose those metrics through the Omni semantic layer, working within the team's governance standards. Rebuild or refresh the core sales dashboards so pipeline reviews and forecasting conversations run on numbers that don't need manual reconciliation. Resolve competing sales metric definitions by writing down the options and tradeoffs, aligning with Sales and RevOps, and documenting the outcome. Model creator-level commerce data, such as products sold, revenue earned through Kit, and conversion, in partnership with data engineering. Write semantic layer descriptions, metric definitions, and documentation that let Omni's AI and MCP-connected AI tools answer routine sales questions correctly, and test them against real questions from Sales, RevOps and Product. Get Sales to the point where routine questions are answered through self-serve, whether in Omni dashboards, Omni's AI, or AI tools connected over MCP, rather than one-off requests.

First Year: Be the recognized owner of Kit's sales data, the person Sales and RevOps go to first and trust without second-guessing. Give our revenue analysis work the sales and commerce models it depends on, so deeper questions about pipeline health and creator earnings can be answered quickly, thereby enabling us to make creators more successful. Leave your domains well documented and well tested, legible to new teammates, stakeholders, and the AI agents that query our semantic layer. Help raise the team's modeling quality through code review, pairing, and sharing what you learn.

Deep dbt experience. You build modular, tested, documented dbt projects, and you're comfortable with incremental models, snapshots, macros, CI, and scheduled jobs in dbt Cloud or similar.

Advanced SQL. You reason carefully about grain, joins, and window functions, and you write queries that are readable and performant on a cloud warehouse.

Data modeling for sales and GTM. You can model deals, pipeline stages and their history, ownership changes, and funnel conversion into clean, reusable structures. You are opinionated on Kimball standards and when a concept should be a fact or dimension.

CRM data fluency. You know how sales data behaves in a CRM, including stage changes, backdated edits, and custom properties. HubSpot is preferred, but other CRM experience (e.g. Salesforce) applies just as well. We care most that you recognize common CRM patterns.

AI-assisted development. You use AI code editors and agents (such as Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, or similar) every day, and you validate their output with tests, query results, and review before anything ships.

Clear written communication. You explain metric definitions, tradeoffs, and data caveats in plain language that Sales, RevOps, and Product partners understand.

BI and semantic layer experience. You can build dashboards and semantic models in Omni, Looker, or a similar BI tool, and you design metric definitions that people and AI tools can both query correctly.

Git-based workflows. You keep pull requests small and well described, and you give thoughtful code review.

Nice to have: Redshift performance tuning (sort and distribution keys), Python for scripting or API pulls, reverse ETL, and experience with product event, billing, payments, or ecommerce data.

Around 7 or more years in analytics engineering or data engineering where you personally shipped production data models.

Several years of hands-on work building and maintaining production dbt projects.

Working directly with sales teams and RevOps, and owning reporting they relied on to run the business.

Owning a data domain over time and evolving its models as the business and its questions changed.

Working at a SaaS company, ideally one with both a self-serve and a sales-led motion. Commerce, payments, or creator economy experience is a plus, not a requirement.

Working on a small or remote team where you were trusted to make decisions independently.

Ownership and follow-through. You treat your data domains as yours. You notice problems before stakeholders do, close the loop on requests, and finish what you start.

Judgment. You match rigor to the decision at hand, know when a quick answer is enough and when a request should become a durable model, and say so clearly.

Flexibility. You're glad to step outside your primary area when another team needs help, and you balance that work against your core commitments openly.

Creator success is our success. You remember who's on the other end. A wrong number in a pipeline review means Sales spends time on the wrong creators, and that shapes how carefully you work.

#7239. JOVS Recruits an Advisory Board to Back Its Beauty Tech

On November 20, JOVS, a beauty tech brand, announced the creation of its Scientific Advisory Board (SAB). This board brings together medical professionals from dermatology, plastic surgery, and physical therapy; and is geared toward making beauty tech more accessible and trustworthy.

The formation of the Scientific Advisory Board also reflects a larger shift in the beauty industry, where many skincare brands have begun marketing products as healthcare as opposed to simply cosmetics.

The company described the board as a long-term collaborative project. It will involve the advisors contributing their expertise to JOVS’ product research and development to ensure that its tech aligns with the wants and needs of consumers.

“Our vision is to make professional beauty tech accessible to everyone, and that is a long-term commitment.” said James Zhang, director of e-commerce and marketing, JOVS. “Self-care is moving away from long, multi-step routines and toward simpler, more effective daily practice, and we want to lead that shift by simplifying complex science without diluting it. The Scientific Advisory Board helps us stay honest to that mission, informing and keeping us accountable to the standards that matter as the brand grows.”

With an increased availability of skincare products, more consumers are seeking out products with substantiated claims beyond heavy marketing. A 2026 survey from YouGov found that 81% of Gen Z shoppers expect ingredient transparency and skincare products with science-backed data.

Many Korean brands have already changed the language of skincare marketing, presenting skincare as a holistic experience with a focus on individual ingredients. Even some makeup products are marketed as having skin benefits, such as blush with “hydrating properties” or “serum” foundations. These products have become integrated into the US beauty scene.

JOVS creates health tech for at-home laser therapy. In 2023, the company released the first FDA-cleared red light therapy mask.

“JOVS has always been built on a simple belief that professional-grade beauty tech should not be confined to clinics.” said Junghong Lin, founder of JOVS. “Our growth has never been about adding more devices to a category. It has been about building the scientific foundation that lets an entire category move forward.”

The brand shared that the Scientific Advisory Board will begin providing insights as the holiday season approaches.

#7224. Product Manager - Postgres at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're hiring a Product Manager to own Postgres itself at Supabase. Every Supabase project is a full Postgres database, and Postgres is the foundation that Auth, Storage, Realtime and Edge Functions run on. You'll own the product experience of the database: Postgres versions and upgrades, maintenance, observability, extensions and security.

You'll work closely with our Postgres engineering teams and with the PM lead for Databases, who leads Multigres. You'll define the Postgres requirements that architecture must meet, and own how customers experience their database through that transition.

This role is ideal for someone who loves Postgres, sweats the details that make a database trustworthy, and is excited about improving the experience for millions of developers. Small improvements here compound across every project on the platform.

You'll own Postgres versions, upgrades and end-of-life. Which major and minor versions we support, how and when customers upgrade, and how we retire old versions. Your goal is to make upgrades routine rather than risky.

You'll make maintenance predictable. You'll design how we schedule, communicate and run maintenance windows so customers stay informed and in control, and disruption is rare and expected.

You'll help developers understand their database. Query performance, metrics, logs and advisors: you'll own the path from "my database is slow" to the fix.

You'll own our extension ecosystem. Which extensions we offer, how we evaluate and support new ones, and how we keep them working across versions.

You'll own Postgres security. Roles and permissions, Row Level Security ergonomics, secure defaults, and how we respond to Postgres and extension CVEs.

You'll talk to developers across the full spectrum, from indie developers on their first project to enterprises running critical workloads, and bring what you learn into every prioritization call.

You'll own the problem definition, requirements and success metrics for what we ship, such as upgrade success rate, the share of the fleet on supported versions, and time-to-diagnose for performance problems. You'll set the metric before the team builds, then track it after launch.

You'll keep Engineering, Support and GTM aligned. You'll own the plan of record for what's coming, why, and in what order, and communicate version changes and deprecations clearly to customers.

4+ years of Product Management experience on databases, developer tools or infrastructure, or equivalent depth as an engineer, DBA or founder moving into product. You're comfortable commenting on an architecture doc and advocating for the user when trade-offs are required.

Know Postgres well, or another relational database well enough to ramp quickly. You're comfortable reading EXPLAIN output and talking about replication, WAL, roles and extensions.

Have shipped products where reliability and trust are the experience. You treat downtime and breaking changes as product problems, not just operational ones.

Write clearly. Your specs, announcements and deprecation notices leave little room for confusion.

Build with what you own. You run queries, test upgrades and try extensions yourself, and form opinions through first-hand use.

Use AI to move faster. You use AI tools to compress research, drafting and feedback synthesis so you spend more time on judgment calls.

Work async by default. Supabase is fully remote. You make decisions in writing and move work forward without meetings.

Ran Postgres in production: upgrades, tuning, incident response.

Worked on database observability, performance tooling, or security and compliance products.

Contributed to Postgres, its extensions, or the wider open-source community.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Curious about the kind of challenges you'll work on at Camunda? Watch this quick 30-minute talk from our engineers to learn more about the new Camunda Exporter and how we’re solving complex problems at scale

We are looking for a Product Builder to join the IDP & RPA pod. You will take real problems from customers, decide what is worth solving, and build and ship the solution yourself, using AI tools to get from idea to production quickly without lowering the bar.

Product Building is how Camunda's Experience organization builds the applications people use on top of Camunda 8, such as Hub, Operate, and ProcessOS. Small pods of builders own a product area from customer problem to shipped change, with no hand-offs between product management, design, and engineering. We are setting this model up now, so you will help shape how it works.

The IDP & RPA pod builds how Camunda processes work with documents and legacy applications. With intelligent document processing (IDP), customers create extraction and classification templates in Camunda Hub and publish them so a process can use AI to turn invoices and other documents into structured data. With robotic process automation (RPA), customers integrate legacy applications that have no APIs, using Robot Framework scripts on a standalone RPA worker. The hard problems come with putting LLMs into customer processes: extraction quality, model and prompt choices, testing and trust, and working for AI agents as well as for people.

Help shape product direction for IDP & RPA by combining customer input, usage data, and field feedback into clear priorities.

Partner with the other builders and product strategy to turn problems into shipped features, using short written proposals and prototypes to align early.

Make judgment calls with incomplete information within your scope and own the outcome.

Communicate trade-offs and decisions clearly to stakeholders who don't live in the details the way you do.

Use AI tools across the whole loop, from specs to prototypes to production-ready code, and hold a high bar for what you ship by reviewing and improving the output rather than accepting it.

Build, test, and ship improvements to the IDP & RPA experience, reading errors and tracing issues far enough to give your pod useful context.

Talk to customers and to support and customer success colleagues about how IDP is used and where it breaks, and turn what you learn into decisions about what to build next.

Pick up customer incidents and trace them across Hub, the connectors, and the deployed process of the customer.

Track what you ship against real outcomes and adjust when the data tells you to.

#7208. Information Security Engineer - Application Security at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Hi there! We're hiring an Information Security Engineer with a focus on Application Security to join our Information Security team at Camunda.

We are a team that takes security seriously without making it feel like a locked door. We work in a transparent, straightforward way, and we care about doing the work right rather than just checking a compliance box.

You will work side by side with our R&D engineers: reviewing designs, threat modeling new features, digging into code and helping teams make good, risk-based decisions about the vulnerabilities that come in. You'll also help build the internal tools that make this work scale, including our CVE registry and the LLM-assisted workflows we use to analyse code and triage findings.

Camunda is an AI-first company, and InfoSec is no exception: AI agents are part of how we review code, assess vulnerabilities, and build our tools. You will help shape how that works.

This is a hands-on role for someone who already has solid AppSec foundations and wants to grow. We are a generalist team, so you will also take part in our on-call rotation and incident response, and get exposure to other areas of security. But most of your time will be spent with engineers and building and maintaining internal tools and processes.

Vulnerability management: triage findings from SCA, container and code scanning, external reports and internal security testing; assess whether Camunda is actually affected; agree fix / mitigate / accept with engineering and follow through to release.

Threat modeling and security reviews: run threat modeling sessions and design reviews with R&D teams for new features and architecture changes, and turn the results into concrete, prioritised actions.

Code analysis with LLMs: use and improve LLM-assisted tooling (agents, prompts, reusable skills) to review code, validate findings and reproduce issues, while applying your own judgement to know when the model is wrong.

Build internal security tooling: contribute to the CVE registry and related automation that connects scanners, engineering teams, and customer communication. Much of it is built on Camunda itself.

Help engineers do the secure thing by default: answer questions, review pull requests, improve guidelines and occasionally run a session or workshop.

On-call and incident response: share the InfoSec on-call rotation with the other engineers and help investigate and resolve security incidents.

Support the wider team: help write security advisories for our customers, answer customer security questions and support audits.

Around 3+ years in security or software engineering, with at least 2 of them working on application or product security (AppSec, product security, pentesting, or a security-focused developer role all count).

Experience at a SaaS or software product company, working closely with engineering teams that ship often through modern CI/CD and cloud environments.

Hands-on vulnerability triage: you've worked with tools like Snyk, Trivy, Semgrep, CodeQL or similar, can tell a real issue from a false positive, and can explain how exploitable something actually is in context (CVSS, exploitability, configuration).

Threat modeling and design review experience (e.g. STRIDE) on real systems, ideally web apps, APIs, or distributed services.

You read and write code. You're comfortable reading Java (Camunda is built on it) well enough to follow a data flow and spot a security bug. You can write scripts and small tools and contribute to an existing codebase with review.

You build with AI, not just chat with it. You use AI coding agents (Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot or similar) in your daily work to write tools, analyse code or automate tasks. And you know their output still needs a second look, especially when it comes to security.

Clear, friendly communication with engineers. You see yourself as an enabler, not a gatekeeper, and you can explain risk to people who aren't security specialists.

Some Python and/or JavaScript experience, or experience building internal tools.

Willingness to learn and use Camunda, since we build a lot of our own security automation on it. Prior BPMN / workflow experience is a plus.

Experience responding to security incidents or being part of an on-call rotation.

Exposure to security advisories, CVE disclosure or bug bounty programmes.

Knowledge of AI/LLM security risks (OWASP Top 10 for LLMs / agentic apps, prompt injection, tool abuse).

Experience securing Kubernetes or container-based workloads, or open-source / B2B software.

#7204. Cadence Health: Registered Nurse - Alerts - Nights

Headquarters: Remote

Cadence is the clinical AI company automating the treatment of chronic disease. Its Clinical Intelligence takes on the routine, high-volume work of chronic care – monitoring patients, surfacing risks, and coordinating action – so clinicians can focus on the decisions that require them. Grounded in partnerships with more than 20 leading health systems, deep EMR integrations, dedicated medical group, and experience serving a population of more than 100,000 patients, the system learns from real-world care delivery, improving as it scales. Cadence has been recognized by TIME as one of the Top 100 HealthTech Companies and by LinkedIn as a Top Startup (#4, 2025).

We're hiring a Registered Nurse to own after-hours patient monitoring, triage, and clinical escalation for Cadence's chronic care population. This is a high-stakes, independent clinical role operating when primary care offices are closed - covering the hours when patients with hypertension, heart failure, and diabetes need a skilled clinician to respond, not a voicemail. You will work directly with patients and the Cadence care team to ensure continuity of care and clinical safety across every after-hours interaction.

Available schedule:

  • Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, and every other Wednesday, 7:00 PM - 7:00 AM 

What You'll Do

  • Triage inbound patient calls and alerts using standardized clinical protocols (including Schmitt-Thompson telephonic triage guidelines), escalating appropriately from after-hours clinic referrals to emergency dispatch when clinical status requires it.
  • Monitor patient vitals, symptoms, and labs in real time to identify patients requiring clinical intervention and execute the appropriate escalation pathway within established care guardrails.
  • Follow up with patients presenting abnormal readings to gather clinical history, assess acuity, and triage to the correct level of care.
  • Document patient case notes in the Cadence platform and log follow-up tasks in ZenDesk to ensure the full care team has a complete clinical record for every after-hours interaction.
  • Respond to inbound patient questions and clinical escalations by phone and text message, maintaining the continuity and trust that define the Cadence care relationship.
  • Conduct outreach to patients with lapses in vital sign adherence and support rescheduling for patients who have missed follow-up touchpoints.
  • Uphold clinical care standards in every patient interaction, reflecting Cadence's commitment to the experience goals of health system partners and the well-being of each patient served.
These responsibilities are intended to describe the general nature and level of work being performed by personnel assigned to this job classification. They are not to be construed as an exhaustive list of job duties performed by personnel in this classification. Other job related duties may be assigned by management.

What You Need

  • Active RN compact (multi-state) licensure required.
  • 4+ years of clinical experience treating patients with CHF, hypertension, and diabetes in an inpatient or outpatient setting.
  • Experience in a CHF bridge clinic or equivalent high-acuity chronic disease management environment.
  • Experience with remote patient monitoring technology and the ability to interpret continuous vital sign data in a clinical context.
  • Demonstrated ability to conduct independent clinical triage and make sound escalation decisions without in-person oversight.
  • Fluency with AI-assisted clinical tools (such as Gemini, Open Evidence, or comparable platforms) and the ability to speak credibly with providers and patients about where AI supports care delivery and where human judgment remains non-negotiable.

Compensation

The anticipated salary range for this role is $80,000 to $90,000, which is based on role scope, level, and location. In addition to base compensation, this role may be eligible for incentive compensation as part of the overall total rewards package. This position is remote and based in the United States. Actual compensation will be determined based on factors including experience, skills, internal equity, and applicable law.

 

Benefits & Perks

  • Competitive pay & equity*
  • Fully remote
  • Comprehensive health coverage: Medical, dental & vision
  • Paid time off
  • 401k plan + matching
  • Paid parental leave
  • Home office stipend

*benefit offerings may vary depending on job profile, job level and worker type

Cadence is committed to equal opportunity and fairness regardless of race, color, religion, sex, gender identity, sexual orientation, nation of origin, ancestry, age, physical or mental disability, country of citizenship, medical condition, marital or domestic partner status, family status, family care status, military or veteran status or any other basis protected by local, state or federal laws.

We're building a community of communities that brings together developers from many backgrounds, including first-time open source contributors. As a DevRel Engineer, you'll be a bridge between Supabase and the broader developer ecosystem, helping people get started, go deep, and feel connected.

#7199. Attorney, New Verticals at Atticus

At any given time, 16 million Americans are experiencing a crisis that requires urgent help from our legal system or government. The right assistance could transform their lives. But today, most never get it.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured person in crisis to get the life-changing aid they deserve. In the last six years, we've become the leading platform connecting people with disabilities to government benefits. We’re also quickly scaling an injury practice to help victims of accidents, misconduct, and violence get compensation from insurance.

So far, we've helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10B in life-changing aid — and earned over 20,000+ five-star reviews. And we're just getting started.

We've raised more than $100 million from top VC firms like Forerunner, GV (Google Ventures), and True Ventures, with ambitions to create a category defining business assisting Americans in need.

Each month, tens of thousands of Americans seek our help for a single legal matter – applying for Social Security Disability, filing a Workers’ Compensation claim – but many of these clients are actually eligible for additional aid without ever knowing it.

Your job as Lead Attorney, New Verticals is to help us do right by these clients, helping them claim additional benefits through the social safety net. You’ll oversee the team of intake specialists who speak with clients who have secondary claims, evaluating the case facts and whether to connect them with an Atticus attorney or local attorney to provide legal advice and potentially take on their case.

This is a job that straddles the line between practicing the law and growing a business. It requires developing a strong understanding of multiple fields of law, and being able to accurately identify when we can help and when we cannot. But even more importantly, this job requires you to be an incredibly strong operator: you need to keep the big picture in mind and stay laser-focused on how we can achieve our goals, oftentimes finding outside-the-box ways to accomplish them. You will report to our General Manager of New Verticals and work closely with our CEO and Managing Attorney.

On any given day, you’ll spend your time:

Speaking with severely injured clients trying to rebuild their lives after a catastrophic accident

Sourcing and vetting local counsel in individual markets, holding a high bar for representation quality and ending relationships that fall below it

Working with our data and insights team to identify existing clients who may have unrecognized claims in adjacent fields of law, and setting the standard for which ancillary cases we take on

Building the playbook for claim types we haven't handled before, starting by screening cases yourself and eventually creating the protocol our intake specialists will run at scale

This is a rare opportunity for an attorney to join a profitable and growing public-interest law firm and take on one of our biggest priorities as a company.

You can understand the nuances of various contingency fields of law and can apply them to a variety of cases (including Social Security Disability, Workers’ Compensation, Personal Injury, and more) – you don’t need to be an expert today, but you should be a fast learner.

You can empathize with anyone and quickly build rapport and relationships while exercising good judgment, both with our clients and local counsel at law firms across the country.

You have strong instincts and judgment as an operator – you can run effective and efficient processes, and you don’t let them run you.

You have a demonstrated commitment to doing good and helping people.

You’ve built a track record as a great people manager – you know how to motivate talent and hold a high bar for output.

You have meaningful work experience outside of serving as a lawyer, ideally at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment (e.g., a business operations team).

We are strongly committed to building a diverse team. If you're from a background that's underrepresented in tech, we'd love to meet you.

This role offers a unique chance to join a startup that has strong traction (substantial funding, well-respected backers, tremendous growth, and many happy customers) but is still small enough that you can have a huge impact and play a role in shaping our success.

We're a certified B Corporation tackling a critical social problem. Our mission to help people in need drives everything we do, and your work here will touch many lives.

We offer competitive pay — including equity — and generous benefits:

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

15 vacation days & ~20 paid holidays each year (including two weeks at end-of-year)

$1,000 reimbursable stipend for education and training outside of work

Up to $1,200/year student loan repayment assistance

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues

We anticipate the base salary band for this role will be between $140,000 and $175,000 in addition to equity and benefits. The salary at offer will be determined by a number of factors such as candidate's experience, knowledge, skills and abilities, as well as internal equity among our team.

This job is fully remote and we’re committed to empowering everyone with flexibility. Work remotely, and travel to LA (on the company dime) as needed to be with your colleagues – usually quarterly, plus offsites. We care a lot about building a great culture and we think some interactions need to happen in person, so we put a lot of thought into retreats, offsites, and other ways to gather.

#7193. Engineering Manager - Pipelines at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Pipelines team at Supabase. Supabase Pipelines is a managed change data capture product. It copies the tables you select from Supabase Postgres to other systems, then streams later changes. The product is in public alpha, with BigQuery, ClickHouse, DuckLake and Snowflake destinations.

Pipelines uses Postgres logical replication and Supabase ETL, our open source replication engine written in Rust. The team works close to the database. That means replication slots, WAL retention, initial table syncs, and the load they place on a customer's source database. The team also builds and runs the destination connectors. Correctness, ordering and recovery matter there as much as speed.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

Build a healthy, high-trust team culture in a remote-first, globally distributed environment.

Run 1:1s, performance management, career development, and onboarding for your direct reports.

Handle people admin (leave, time off, etc.) for your team.

Own the hiring plan for your team and act as Hiring Manager. First round of interviewer for all roles on the team.

Partner with Talent Acquisition to source, interview, and close strong candidates.

Contribute to headcount planning, org design, and the evolution of engineering leadership at Supabase.

Own the team's roadmap and project management (Linear), driving accountability and clear milestones.

Manage workflow intake and Slack triage. These are the team’s primary inboxes for cross-team requests. You decide what gets routed where, prioritized, or pushed back on.

Own the team's weekly updates and keep stakeholders aligned without creating overhead.

Organize effort against support tickets and maintain the team's SLO.

Surface risks, dependencies, and tradeoffs early and clearly to leadership.

Train as an Incident Commander. Drive coordination, resolution, and post-incident follow-up.

Have 5+ years of engineering background, with enough depth to be credible with engineers building complex systems.

Have 3+ years managing remote engineering teams.

Have managed a team that built and ran data pipelines, change data capture, replication or streaming systems.

Have strong technical credibility in data systems. We look for delivery guarantees, ordering, idempotency, schema changes and backfills.

Know Postgres replication well, including logical replication, the WAL and replication slots, and how they affect a production database.

Know analytical destinations such as BigQuery, Snowflake, ClickHouse or lakehouse table formats like Iceberg.

Have led a team through production incidents on a system that holds customer data. You ran the follow-up work and kept the review blameless.

Have shipped a developer-facing data product to customers outside your company.

Have experience with Rust (not required but a plus)

Have managed remote, globally distributed teams that work asynchronously across disparate time zones.

Are technical enough to be credible with strong engineers, but genuinely passionate about people management. This is the work you want to do, not a track you're settling for.

Build psychological safety and a feedback culture. You give feedback often and in the moment, ask for it back, and create space for the team to do the same.

Operate as a multiplier. You help individuals achieve more than they could alone, and you don't need to be the best technical voice in the room.

Coach rather than direct. You remove blockers, connect people to context, and trust your engineers to own how the work gets done.

Have a real track record of performance management and can speak to specific examples of difficult conversations.

Are comfortable with ambiguity and can build structure where there isn't much.

Communicate clearly and write well. You default to async and written, set context early, and surface risks early.

Are collaborative, low-ego and adaptable. You thrive with high-autonomy senior engineers who don't need you to be the smartest person in the room.

#7191. Engineering Manager - Realtime at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Realtime team at Supabase. Realtime lets developers send low-latency messages between clients, track and synchronize user state, and listen to database changes. Those three features are Broadcast, Presence and Postgres Changes. Broadcast also sends from the database and over HTTP, and clients can replay recent messages.

Realtime is a globally distributed Elixir cluster built on Phoenix. The team balances low latency, fair limits between projects, and the cost of running the service. Customers expect connections to stay up, so reliability matters as much as new features.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code, but you you will lead a team of experienced engineers. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

#7188. Grafana Labs: Engineering Manager - Grafana Infrastructure (Remote, Spain)

Grafana Labs is the company behind Grafana Cloud, the fully managed observability platform trusted by more than 10,000 organizations to ensure reliability, resolve incidents faster, and optimize telemetry at scale. Built on open source and open standards and designed for interoperability across any stack, Grafana Cloud brings AI to observability and observability to AI, giving teams (and their agents) unified visibility so they can see, understand, and act on all their disparate data, wherever it lives, and move at the speed of their ambitions. Customers, including Anthropic, Bloomberg, NVIDIA, Microsoft, and Salesforce, rely on Grafana Labs. We are a 100% remote company with team members across 40+ countries, backed by leading investors including Lightspeed Venture Partners, Sequoia Capital, GIC, Coatue, J.P. Morgan, CapitalG, and Lead Edge Capital. Learn more at grafana.com and follow us on LinkedIn and X.

We’re scaling fast and staying true to what makes us different: an open-source legacy, a global collaborative culture, and a passion for meaningful work. Our team thrives in an innovation-driven environment where transparency, autonomy, and trust fuel everything we do.

You may not meet every requirement, and that’s okay. If this role excites you, we’d love you to raise your hand for what could be a truly career-defining opportunity.

This is a remote position and we're considering candidates in the UK, Spain or Ireland.

Grafana Cloud moves millions of metrics, log lines, and traces per second from our customers' environments into a highly available, low-latency stack that processes and stores this data, and serves it to dashboards and alerting tools. We aim to grow this to hundreds of millions per second, and it's critical that as we grow, we improve our performance, increase our reliability, and do it efficiently and securely.

The Foundations department owns the Internal Engineering Platform (IEP): the Kubernetes clusters, cloud service provider resources (CSP), hardware, identity, delivery, and observability systems that Grafana Labs's application engineers use to build, deploy, and run their workloads.

We're hiring an engineering manager for the Foundations Infrastructure group, which owns the cloud infrastructure Grafana Cloud runs on. The group provisions and operates Kubernetes clusters and CSP resources across GCP, AWS, and Azure; enforces identity, access, and security controls across those environments; and runs the observability and cost-visibility systems that keep the infrastructure measurable and accountable.

You'll lead one of the squads of platform engineers that comprise the group.

We're platform engineers, and our application teams are our users. Our job is to give them leverage: self-service building blocks that take care of the hard parts of infrastructure, so they can focus on what they're building. We treat the platform as a product and value the experience of the people who use it. When a change is well communicated and understood, teams welcome it, and it lands as an improvement that makes the right way the easy way. You'll lead a squad that makes that happen.

Foundations is a global team that works asynchronously. The people who own a component or problem make the decisions about it, once stakeholders have had a chance to weigh in. Application teams build and run their own services; we give them a self-service platform to do it on.

Every engineer is on-call for the components their squad provides. We plan on-call, tactical work, and time off into every quarter, so roadmap work is roughly half of an engineer's time rather than all of it.

We invest heavily in developer productivity. You can use modern AI coding assistants as part of your daily workflow (your choice of tools, within security guidelines), backed by a company-funded usage budget so you can iterate quickly without unnecessary friction.

We encourage pragmatic AI-assisted development: faster prototyping, test generation, refactors, documentation, and incident follow-ups, always paired with strong code review and quality standards.

In Spain, the base compensation range for this role is €96,846 - €116,215. Actual compensation may vary based on level, experience, and skillset as assessed throughout the interview process. All of our roles include Restricted Stock Units (RSUs), giving every team member ownership in Grafana Labs' success. We believe in shared outcomes—RSUs help us stay aligned and invested as we scale globally.

*Compensation ranges are country specific. If you are applying for this role from a different location than listed above, your recruiter will discuss your specific market’s defined pay range & benefits at the beginning of the process.

Equal Opportunity Employer:Grafana Labs is an equal opportunities employer. We welcome applications from everyone regardless of race, colour, nationality, origin, caste, sex, gender reassignment identity or expression, sexual orientation, age, religion or belief, disability, veteran status, genetic information, pregnancy, maternity, marital, family or carer status, or any other characteristic which is protected by local law.
